Well, sir... I guess maybe a little bit? I really try to be clear, I promise I do. I don't want to confuse anyone on purpose. 

But sometimes, when I'm trying to explain things, it just all comes out at once. I mean, just the normal aches and pains, you know, getting older and all. But no doctor ever told me I had a real illness before all this... all this trouble started. I just want to be helpful.\n\nDoctor: About how long have you been feeling sure that your neighbors and the apartment office are watching you?\n\nPatient: Well, sir... I guess... it's been a little while now. I'd say right around a year and a month. Give or take. I really hope that doesn't make me sound too... you know, difficult or anything. I've tried so hard to just keep my head down and not make a fuss the whole time, I really have. \n\nBut yes, about a year and a month since I started noticing it all. I probably should have talked to someone sooner, I just... I didn't want to be a bother to anybody, you know? Am I doing okay explaining this for you? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right timeline.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Well, sir... I guess... yes, I have. I really hate to admit it, I don't want to sound like a complainer or anything. I know people have it a lot worse than me. But... yeah. For a while now, actually. \n\nMost mornings, I wake up with this... this heavy, hollow feeling. It's just sitting right there in my chest. It's like the day already went bad before I even get my feet on the floor. I try to shake it off, I really do, but it just kind of stays with me. I hope it's okay to say that? I'm not trying to be negative, it's just... it's been really hard to feel good about much of anything lately. Am I explaining that alright for you?\n\nDoctor: When you were not feeling depressed, did you still believe your neighbors and the apartment office were watching you?\n\nPatient: Well, sir... I was trying so hard to be positive.\n\nBut... well, I hate to say it, but even when my mood was better, I... I still knew they were watching my door. The neighbors, I mean, and the office. I just get so nervous trying to talk to people normally. I don't trust how they look at me. \n\nI'm really sorry, I know I should be doing better. Well, sir... I guess... I mean, maybe a little bit? I hope you don't think badly of me for this. I really always try to do my best and be helpful.

I know the people there are... well, they're watching my comings and goings. And they've been slipping notes about me to the office. \n\nEverybody keeps telling me it's just, you know, regular notices taped to the doors for everyone, but... I know what's going on. I hope it's okay I'm bringing this up? I just want to do whatever I'm supposed to do to get things straightened out.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one else is around?\n\nPatient: No, I... I haven't been hearing anything like that. Just the usual noises, you know, from the neighbors and the street. I really hope I'm not giving you the wrong impression, I'm not... I'm not hearing voices or anything. Is it okay that I just hear the regular apartment sounds? I just want to make sure I'm answering you right.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe for certain that your neighbors and the apartment office are deliberately watching you?\n\nPatient: Well... I really don't want you to think badly of me, Doctor. I know how it sounds. But... yes, sir. I really do. \n\nLike I was saying before, I know they're keeping track of when I leave and come back, and passing those notes to the office. People keep telling me I'm wrong and that they're just... you know, regular flyers taped to everyone's doors, but I just know what's really going on. I'm not trying to cause any trouble for anyone there, I promise. I always keep to myself. I just... I hope it's okay that I'm being honest about this with you? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers so I'm doing what I'm supposed to do.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually energetic or high, like you needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: No, sir. I've never had anything like that happen. I... I usually feel pretty tired, honestly. I sleep as much as I can. I don't ever get, you know, worked up or overly energetic like that. I hope that's the right answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to know. I try to stay pretty calm and quiet, I really do.\n\nDoctor: Before these concerns about your neighbors started, had you been diagnosed with any medical or neurological illness?\n\nPatient: No, sir. I... I haven't had anything like that. I've always been pretty healthy, far as I know. No big medical problems or... or brain things, if that's what you mean. I mean, just the normal aches and pains, you know, getting older and all. But no doctor ever told me I had a real illness before all this... all this trouble started. Is that what you were looking for? I hope I'm answering that right for you. I just want to be helpful.\n\nDoctor: About how long have you been feeling sure that your neighbors and the apartment office are watching you?\n\nPatient: Well, sir... I guess... it's been a little while now. I'd say right around a year and a month. Give or take. I really hope that doesn't make me sound too... you know, difficult or anything. I've tried so hard to just keep my head down and not make a fuss the whole time, I really have. \n\nBut yes, about a year and a month since I started noticing it all. I probably should have talked to someone sooner, I just... I didn't want to be a bother to anybody, you know? Am I doing okay explaining this for you? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right timeline.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Well, sir... I guess... yes, I have. I really hate to admit it, I don't want to sound like a complainer or anything. I know people have it a lot worse than me. But... yeah. For a while now, actually. \n\nMost mornings, I wake up with this... this heavy, hollow feeling. It's just sitting right there in my chest. It's like the day already went bad before I even get my feet on the floor. I try to shake it off, I really do, but it just kind of stays with me. I hope it's okay to say that? I'm not trying to be negative, it's just... it's been really hard to feel good about much of anything lately. Am I explaining that alright for you?\n\nDoctor: When you were not feeling depressed, did you still believe your neighbors and the apartment office were watching you?\n\nPatient: Well, sir... I guess... yes. I mean, there was a spell a little while back, maybe for a few weeks, where I actually felt a little lighter. I was eating a bit better, and I wasn't waking up with that awful heavy feeling quite so much. I really thought I was turning a corner, I did. I was trying so hard to be positive.\n\nBut... well, I hate to say it, but even when my mood was better, I... I still knew they were watching my door. The neighbors, I mean, and the office. I still knew they were keeping tabs on me and reporting things. I just couldn't shake that part of it, even when I felt okay otherwise. I'm sorry, I know that probably sounds strange. I hope I'm not making things too complicated for you. Is that what you needed to know?\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you found it much harder to keep up with your usual daily activities, like work, chores, or taking care of yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, sir... I guess... yes, it has been a bit of a struggle lately. I really try my best to keep on top of things, I honestly do. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around being lazy or anything like that. \n\nBut, um... it's just gotten so confusing. I've... I've fallen a bit behind on some of my bills, and I missed a couple of appointments. I feel terrible about it, I really do. I hate letting people down. And I... I haven't been going to church or my volunteer work like I used to. It's just... it's so hard to keep my days straight right now, and with everyone watching... I just get so nervous trying to talk to people normally. I don't trust how they look at me. \n\nI'm really sorry, I know I should be doing better. I hope you don't think less of me for letting things slip like this. I'm trying to get back on track, I promise. Does that make sense? I hope I'm answering your questions the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your emotions feel flat or that it’s hard to show facial expression or feelings when you talk with people?\n\nPatient: Well, sir... I don't... I don't think I've had that problem. I hope that's an alright answer. \n\nI mean, I feel like I still show my feelings okay. I always try my best to smile and be polite when I have to talk to someone, even if I'm feeling pretty low inside. I really wouldn't want anyone to think I was being rude or cold to them, you know? I haven't had anyone tell me my face looks blank or anything like that, anyway. \n\nI hope I'm not missing something you're looking for by saying no. I can try to pay more attention to it if you think I should? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. Am I doing okay with these questions?\n\nDoctor: Have other people told you that your speech is hard to follow or that you jump from one topic to another?\n\nPatient: Well, sir... I guess maybe a little bit? I really try to be clear, I promise I do. I don't want to confuse anyone on purpose. \n\nBut sometimes, when I'm trying to explain things, it just all comes out at once. Like, I'll start talking about the rent, and then I'll mention the marina, and then somehow I'm talking about the choir robes and... and the wires in the walls. And I'll see people looking at me like I skipped half the story, or like they don't know how I got from one thing to the other. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know it must be frustrating for them, and probably for you too. I just have so much on my mind, and it all feels connected to me, but I guess it doesn't sound that way to anyone else. I really hope I'm making sense right now. Am I explaining it okay? I can try to slow down if you need me to.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself talking much less than you used to, even when you want to say more?\n\nPatient: Well, sir... no, I don't think I've had that happen. I hope that's the right answer. \n\nI mean, I think I still talk about the same amount as I always have. Maybe even a little more sometimes, when I get nervous and start rambling... like I was just saying before. I really try to make sure I answer people when they talk to me, and I wouldn't want to just sit there and ignore them. That would be so rude, and I really try hard to be polite. \n\nI haven't noticed myself just... stopping talking, or not being able to say what I want. I hope I'm not disappointing you by not having that problem. I just want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need to hear. Am I still doing okay?\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when your behavior seemed very disorganized or odd to other people?\n\nPatient: Well, sir... I guess... I mean, maybe a little bit? I hope you don't think badly of me for this. I really always try to do my best and be helpful. \n\nBut... well, I go to this workshop at the senior center sometimes, just to help out, you know? And the other day, I was supposed to be fixing this shelf. But instead of actually fixing it, I just... I got really focused on the screws. I kept lining them all up by size, perfectly straight, and then I was just tapping the table. I didn't mean to be odd, I promise. It just felt like the right thing to do in that moment. \n\nAnd then the lady there, she asked me to just sit down. But I couldn't seem to... I don't know, switch gears? I just stood there holding the screwdriver for a long time. I think it probably looked really strange to her. I felt so bad about it afterward. I really didn't want to cause any trouble or make anyone uncomfortable. \n\nIs that the kind of thing you were asking about? I hope I'm answering these right. I just want to be a good patient for you, sir.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, sir... I suppose I have been, yes. I hope you don't think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself, because I really do try to keep a good attitude. I don't ever want to come across as ungrateful or like I'm just complaining to you. 

But... well, if I'm being completely honest, most mornings I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling inside. It's almost like... like the day already went bad before I even get my feet on the floor. I try my very best to push through it and put on a brave face so I don't bother anybody, but it's been there for most of the year, I think. 

I hope it's alright that I'm admitting that to you. I feel a little ashamed that I can't just snap out of it on my own, but I want to give you the honest truth so I can be a good patient. Does that answer your question the way you need me to, sir?

I've fallen a bit behind on some of my bills, and I missed a couple of appointments. I feel terrible about it, I really do. I hate letting people down. And I... Well, sir... I guess I have to say yes to that, too. I really hate to admit it, because I know how it sounds, and I promise I'm not trying to be difficult or lazy. I hope you know I've always tried to keep busy and be useful.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Well, sir... I guess I'd have to say yes to that. I really hope you don't think I'm neglecting my health on purpose, because I know how important it is to take care of myself. I don't want you to worry about me or think I'm just giving up.
